soupOS — Feature Roadmap
========================
A 32-bit hobby OS written in C and NASM.
Current version: v0.60.284

Features are grouped roughly by effort. Nothing here is required;
everything is just "it would be funny/cool to build."

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CHANGELOG
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

v0.60.284 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM: :wq OTHER DOES NOT QUIT A CHANGED BUFFER
  - v0.60.278 fixed :w OTHER but not the quits: :wq OTHER (and :x OTHER)
    on a changed named buffer wrote OTHER and quit, the buffer's own file
    never getting the edit (review R-N30). They write OTHER and then, the
    buffer still changed, refuse to quit with E37, as :q does.
  - neosim-writeas-test: a second boot, x and :wq /C.TXT: the copy is
    written and neosim stays; :wq then writes /A.TXT. Before the fix it
    quit with /A.TXT unchanged.

v0.60.283 (2026-10-12)  — THE WRITE BUFFERS SHARE ONE BUDGET
  - v0.60.275 capped each write handle at the disk's room, but each one
    alone: five at once took 28 MB apiece and memory ran out under them
    (review R-K24, my K4b incomplete). One count of the pages every write
    buffer holds is kept now, capped at the disk's room as last measured,
    all that could ever reach it: at -m 128M the buffers can no longer
    take the machine's memory. The append path's failed load now gives
    its pages back too (it leaked them).
  - scripts/writebudget-test.sh: five background spoons write /dev/zero
    into five files at once: none runs out of memory, the shared cap
    stops them at 28450816 bytes between them, the shell answers. Before
    the fix the fifth ran out of memory.

v0.60.282 (2026-10-12)  — CTCOMPOSTMODE-TEST CHECKS ALL THREE MODES
  - It guarded only stream_copy's mode: with the compost bowl's chmod or
    .whence's taken out it still passed, though saucier could then list
    the headchef's composted files (review R-T29). And its one mode check
    read the last perms: line, which, with the bowl closed to saucier,
    could be the headchef's own earlier one. Now saucier's cd /compost
    must be refused and no listing show priv.txt, and the headchef, back
    in, reads the three modes: the bowl rwx---, the file rw----, the index
    rw----. With both chmods taken out every one of those fails.

v0.60.281 (2026-10-12)  — TOPRAM-TEST CAN FAIL AGAIN
  - v0.60.275's write cap stops /dev/zero > /z at the disk's room (28 MB),
    short of the top frames at -m 128M: with v0.60.268's map fix taken
    out, topram-test still passed (review R-T28). It boots -m 32M now,
    where free RAM is under the disk's room, and requires the buffer's
    "out of memory at" line so it cannot go quiet again. With the fix
    taken out it panics at 0x1f7f000 and fails; with it, it passes.

v0.60.280 (2026-10-12)  — SOUPYC'S REMOVE ASKS WHOSE FILE
  - v0.60.262 put soupyc's opens behind the cook's rights but missed
    remove(), which called fat_delete with nothing asked, in every build:
    saucier removed /etc/kitchen, and the next boot reseeded the headchef
    with the stock secret and dropped every hired cook (review R-K21,
    critical, the second full review's). In the ordinary build it asks
    what strain asks now: 'w' on the file, and no bowls.
  - soupyc-perms-test: saucier's remove("/etc/kitchen") gives 0 and the
    file stays; saucier's own file still removes. Before the fix it gave 1
    and /etc/kitchen was gone.

v0.60.279 (2026-10-12)  — CTSLOTS-TEST READS PAST KERNEL LINES
  - Under the full check's load a kernel line landed in the middle of
    "prod: hog holds 8", and ctslots-test read no number ("the hog held
    none"; alone it passed twice). It takes the kernel's lines out before
    reading what the program said, as prodwin-test does.

v0.60.278 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM: :w OTHER KEEPS THE BUFFER'S NAME
  - :w OTHER took OTHER as the buffer's name and cleared [+], as vim does
    only for a buffer with no name: after x and :w /B.TXT, :q quit with
    the edit never written to the file it came from (review R-N3). A named
    buffer keeps its name now and only a write to it clears [+]; a buffer
    with no name still takes the name written.
  - scripts/neosim-writeas-test.sh: x, :w /B.TXT, :q refused (E37), :wq
    writes /A.TXT; both hold the edit. Before the fix :q quit and /A.TXT
    kept its old text.

v0.60.277 (2026-10-12)  — AN OVERWRITE THAT FAILS KEEPS THE OLD FILE
  - fat_write freed an existing file's chain before writing the new one.
    A write that then ran out of room had given the old clusters away,
    written part of the new data into them and left the entry pointing at
    a free chain: the old file was gone and fsck complained (review R-M4:
    a failed Mincecraft save lost the world it replaced). The new chain is
    written first now and the old one freed only after the entry points
    at the new, so an overwrite needs room for both for that moment.
  - scripts/overwrite-safe-test.sh: decant a 60 KB file over a 10 KB one
    with 20 KB free: the copy fails, the old file is whole (cmp), fsck is
    clean. Before the fix it read 0 bytes over a free cluster.

v0.60.276 (2026-10-12)  — MINCECRAFT: FLYING HAS A CEILING
  - Flying had none, but a world file with the cook's feet at y 68 (WY +
    4) or above is refused on load, and the next start made a new world
    in its place: everything built was lost (review R-M1). The feet stop
    at y 66 now, two blocks over the top row, inside what a save keeps.
  - scripts/mincecraft-ceiling-test.sh: fly on, Space held 14 s, P: the
    cook stops at 66.00 and the next boot loads the world. Before the fix
    it climbed to 82.55 and the world was not loaded.

v0.60.275 (2026-10-12)  — A WRITE BUFFER HOLDS NO MORE THAN THE DISK COULD TAKE
  - A file opened for writing is buffered in fresh frames until it is
    closed, and nothing capped it: cook spoon.elf /dev/zero > /z took
    121 MB, every free frame, before its write to a 32 MB volume failed,
    and every other user of memory starved meanwhile (review R-K4, its
    second half; v0.60.268 had stopped the panic). A write handle now
    stops at the volume's free space plus what the file it replaces holds,
    set at its first write: past it the write fails and the log says
    where (28452864 bytes on the stock disk).
  - scripts/writecap-test.sh: 15 s of that, Ctrl-C: the buffer stopped at
    the disk's room, memory never ran out, the shell answers. Before the
    fix memory ran out at 121348096 bytes.

v0.60.274 (2026-10-12)  — A SPAWNED SCRIPT READS NO KEYS
  - soupyc's input reads its terminal's getc, which on the console is the
    keyboard itself, with no foreground rule: a spawn()ed script raced the
    shell for every key, and one left running after clockout raced the
    next login (review R-K17, the /dev/kbd hole of v0.60.264 by another
    door). A spawned script's input is an empty line now (a task with its
    uid set is a spawned one); the foreground script's is as before.
  - scripts/soupyc-spawn-input-test.sh: a spawned worker's input is empty
    and the shell has the whole word typed after. Before the fix the
    worker printed GOT [arsnipword] and the shell lost it.

v0.60.273 (2026-10-12)  — ONE BIG SYSCALL PAST THE CAP IS SAFE, AND CHEAP
  - v0.60.269 faulted every page of a syscall's buffer in at its top. A
    buffer past the page cap evicted its own first pages doing it, could
    take the last swap slot, and the copy into page 0 then needed swap_in,
    which made room (a free slot) BEFORE freeing the page's own: none,
    and a panic (review R-K15, reproduced: one read into 5114 fresh pages).
    And a 20 MB read of a 47-byte file mapped, zeroed and swapped the
    whole buffer for nothing (R-K16).
  - user_ok touches nothing now: it counts the new pages the range could
    need (neither mapped nor on disk) and refuses the call (-1) only if
    the room under the cap, the free swap slots and the free frames could
    not cover them. swap_in reads the page in and frees its slot before it
    evicts, so a page on disk always comes back; swap_out_one takes its
    slot before taking the page out of its set (a full swap used to drop a
    resident page from it). Another program taking the last slots between
    the count and the copy is not covered.
  - proof.elf kbig N; kfill-test now also makes one read into as many
    fresh pages as kfill could have: 47 bytes, no panic. On v0.60.269 it
    panicked at 0xc4000000.

v0.60.272 (2026-10-12)  — SOUPYC'S INCLUDE FINDS ITS FILE
  - include copied its file name from tok.sval, but the lexer keeps a
    string literal in tok.str (tok.sval is cleared for every token), so
    the name was always empty and every include said "file not found":
    the disk's own INCLUDE.SC never ran, in any build, and no test ran it
    (review R-K20); soupyc-perms-test's include case could not fail
    (R-T25). The name comes from tok.str now.
  - scripts/soupyc-include-test.sh: INCLUDE.SC prints cube(3) = 27 from
    MATHLIB.SC; saucier's include of /MATHLIB.SC works and of /etc/kitchen
    is refused, nothing of it printed.

v0.60.271 (2026-10-12)  — KBDTAP-TEST TESTS ONLY WHAT IT CAN
  - kbdtap-test's SSH half passed with v0.60.264's dev_kbd_read guard
    taken out: the shell's pour opens only files on the disk ("Not found:
    /dev/kbd"), so it never reached the device, and no reader on another
    terminal that does was found (review R-T24). That half is gone; the
    guard stays as a backstop, and its comment and CLAUDE.md say it has
    no known caller and no test. The soupyc half (open gives -1) is what
    the test checks.

v0.60.270 (2026-10-12)  — A COMPOSTED FILE KEEPS ITS MODE
  - The desktop's compost, copy and put back made every new file rwxr-x,
    whatever the old one's mode, and the headchef's compost is /compost
    at the root, made rwxr-x too: a rw---- file the headchef composted was
    every cook's to read, and /compost/.whence named where each came from
    (review R-C16, the second area review's). stream_copy now gives the
    copy its source's mode; the compost bowl is made (and kept) rwx---,
    its .whence rw----. Put back still makes the file the desktop cook's.
  - scripts/ctcompostmode-test.sh: the headchef composts a rw---- file;
    saucier is refused it and the index, and it reads rw----. Before the
    fix saucier read both.

v0.60.269 (2026-10-12)  — A SYSCALL'S COPY THE PAGER CANNOT BACK IS A -1
  - user_ok passed any heap page below the break (and any stack page above
    the guard) as one the demand pager would give, but at the page cap
    with the swap full it gives none: the kernel's own copy into it faulted
    in ring 0, the pager declined, and the machine panicked (review R-K3).
    Such a page is faulted in at the syscall's top now, where nothing is
    held, and refused if the pager cannot back it: the call gives -1.
  - proof.elf kfill: 32 MB, every page written by the kernel through
    sys_random. scripts/kfill-test.sh: refused at page 5114 of 8192 (the
    cap and the swap), no panic, the shell answers. Before the fix it
    panicked at 0xc53fa000.

v0.60.268 (2026-10-12)  — EVERY FRAME PMM GIVES IS MAPPED
  - The kernel's identity map ran to pmm_total_pages(), a count of usable
    pages, not the highest: below it the hole at 0xA0000-0xFFFFF left ~97
    frames at the top of RAM (from 0x7f7f000 at -m 128M) free in pmm and
    mapped nowhere, and the first kernel write to one panicked. Any cook
    got there in 7 s with cook spoon.elf /dev/zero > /z, which buffers
    the file in fresh frames (review R-K4, its first half). pmm keeps one
    past the highest usable page now (pmm_top_page) and the map runs to
    it. The same run now fills memory, fails its write to the disk, is
    killed, and every page comes back. That the write buffer has no cap
    is still open.
  - scripts/topram-test.sh: 20 s of that, Ctrl-C, no panic, the shell
    answers. Before the fix it panicked at 0x7f7f000.

v0.60.267 (2026-10-12)  — THE DESKTOP ASKS WHOSE PROGRAM UNDER ITS LOCK
  - SYS_WIN_OPEN and SYS_NOTE asked ct_admits before taking app_mtx: a
    call made just as the desktop came up could see ct_running 0, wait on
    the mutex while the start set it to 1, and take a slot unchecked, the
    v0.60.259 hole again for one window (review R-C12). The question is
    asked inside the mutex now, in both. No test makes the race happen (it
    needs the desktop preempted inside its few locked lines at start);
    ctforeign-test still passes.

v0.60.266 (2026-10-12)  — A SPAWNED SCRIPT KEEPS ITS COOK
  - A spawn()ed soupyc script runs as a kernel task with no program, so
    v0.60.258's rule (a program's own cook) missed it and its rights were
    its shell's current cook: one left running after clockout opened
    files as the next cook, /etc/kitchen included once the headchef
    clocked in (review R-K14, worse since v0.60.262 made soupyc ask at
    all). A task now has a uid, -1 unless set; the spawned script's is the
    spawner's cook, set before it runs a line, and the resolver asks it
    after a program's owner.
  - scripts/soupyc-spawn-rights-test.sh: saucier's worker waits for a file
    the headchef makes after clocking in; /etc/kitchen is refused and
    saucier's own file opens. Before the fix it opened both.

v0.60.265 (2026-10-12)  — WINDOW SLOTS DO NOT OUTLIVE A DESKTOP
  - Leave marks a live program's windows gone, so its calls get -1, but
    kept their slots and their pixels in the kernel's heap, and the next
    desktop freed only slots of programs that had ended: one program left
    running with eight windows (there are eight slots) shut every later
    cook out of program windows until a reboot, and seven at 640x480 held
    7.2 MB of heap (review R-C10). The desktop's start frees every gone
    slot now. A cap on slots per program is not taken yet.
  - prod.elf hog: every slot it can get, held while it sleeps.
  - scripts/ctslots-test.sh: saucier's hog takes all eight, saucier leaves
    and clocks out, and the headchef's prod.elf victim still gets its
    window. Before the fix it got none.

v0.60.264 (2026-10-12)  — /DEV/KBD IS NOT SOUPYC'S
  - v0.60.257 sent a program's /dev/kbd reads through the stdin rule, but
    soupyc's open("/dev/kbd") still reached dev_kbd_read, with no
    foreground rule, and a spawn()ed soupyc task outlives a shift: the
    next cook's keys, secrets typed at the login included (review R-K13).
    soupyc may not open the keyboard now, in any build (input() is its
    keyboard), and dev_kbd_read gives EOF to a task on any terminal but
    the console's.
  - scripts/kbdtap-test.sh: soupyc's open("/dev/kbd") gives -1 (0 before
    the fix), and pour /dev/kbd in an SSH session ends at once.

v0.60.263 (2026-10-12)  — NO COMPOST FOR A FILE THE COOK MAY NOT READ
  - Removing a file in the files window copies it into the cook's own
    ~/compost, to come back, and only 'w' on it was asked: a file the
    cook might write but not read became theirs to read (the headchef's
    /drop/memo.txt, rw--w-, read by saucier after "remove"; review R-C2).
    Such a file is refused now, left where it is, with a note on the bar;
    strain at a prompt still deletes it, with no copy made.
  - scripts/ctcompostread-test.sh: saucier removes it in the files window
    (asked twice); refused, still in /drop, nothing in saucier's compost.
    Before the fix it was composted there.

v0.60.262 (2026-10-12)  — SOUPYC'S FILES OBEY THE COOK'S RIGHTS
  - soupyc runs in ring 0 on the shell's task, and its open(), lines(),
    write_lines() and include called vfs_open with nothing asked: in the
    ordinary build any cook read /etc/kitchen with soup -c (the
    headchef's hash, where pour said Permission denied) and wrote
    anywhere (review R-K12, critical; the area review's). They go through
    sc_open now, which asks users_may_open, SYS_OPEN's rule moved into
    users.c so both ask the same: 'r' to read, 'w' on the file or the bowl
    it would be made in to write. The CHALLENGE build keeps its unchecked
    open, the stage 1 it teaches.
  - scripts/soupyc-perms-test.sh: as saucier, open and lines of
    /etc/kitchen, open for writing and write_lines in / are all refused
    and nothing is made; saucier's own file still reads. Before the fix
    every one went through (OPEN 0, LINES 3, WLINES 1).

v0.60.261 (2026-10-12)  — DEVKBD-TEST'S BACKGROUND HALF CAN FAIL
  - A background spoon that steals the console's keys never ends, so its
    /KEYS.TXT was never written, and "nothing in it" passed (review
    R-T20). Now the background spoon must have ended before the word is
    typed, /KEYS.TXT must be there and empty, and the shell must have had
    the whole word; a failed drive no longer hides those checks behind
    "the machine stopped answering". With the foreground check taken out
    of user_stdin_getchar all three fail and say so.

v0.60.260 (2026-10-12)  — CLOCKOUT-RIGHTS-TEST CANNOT PASS BY TIMING
  - infuse.elf waited a fixed 12 s, so on a slow login it woke at the
    login screen, was refused there, and the test passed on a kernel
    without the v0.60.258 fix (review R-T19). It is infuse.elf GOPATH PATH
    now: it waits for GOPATH to exist, and the test makes /GO only once
    the headchef has clocked in (after a deliberate pause before the
    secret); a refusal before the headchef's Welcome is a FAIL of its own.
    With the resolver fix taken out the test fails; with it, it passes.

v0.60.259 (2026-10-12)  — NO WINDOWS FROM ANOTHER COOK'S PROGRAMS
  - A program's window goes on top and takes the focus, so it gets the
    desktop's keys, and nothing asked whose program it was: any cook's,
    from an SSH session or left running from an earlier shift, could open
    one on another cook's desktop and log their typing, and SYS_NOTE wrote
    on their bar (review R-C1). SYS_WIN_OPEN and SYS_NOTE now pass the
    caller's cook, and the desktop takes only its own cook's programs
    (none before a clock-in); a refusal is logged once a pid.
  - scripts/ctforeign-test.sh: saucier's background prod.elf keys, left
    waiting for a desktop, is refused on the headchef's; the headchef's
    own still gets its window. Before the fix saucier's got one.

v0.60.258 (2026-10-12)  — A PROGRAM HAS ITS OWN COOK'S RIGHTS
  - A program's open, unlink, mkdir, readdir, stat and kill were decided
    by its shell's current cook, which clockout changes: a program left
    running had the next cook's rights, and saucier's background program
    read the headchef's line of /etc/kitchen once the headchef clocked in
    (review R-K2, critical). Inside a program's syscall the uid is now the
    program's own owner, set at spawn. Programs left running still are
    not killed at clockout: what they can do is their own cook's.
  - infuse.elf SECONDS PATH, a test fixture: waits, then opens PATH and
    prints its first line or INFUSE refused.
  - scripts/clockout-rights-test.sh: saucier's two background infuse.elf
    wake after the headchef has clocked in; /etc/kitchen is refused and
    saucier's own file still read. Before the fix the hash was printed.

v0.60.257 (2026-10-12)  — /DEV/KBD NO LONGER FREEZES THE MACHINE
  - A program's read of /dev/kbd ran dev_kbd_read inside the syscall,
    where interrupts are off: keyboard_getchar's hlt never woke, and any
    cook could freeze the whole machine (cook spoon.elf /dev/kbd, then a
    key). It had no foreground check either, so a background or SSH
    program would have read the console's keys, secrets typed at the
    login and at chef included (review R-K1, critical). SYS_READ now reads
    /dev/kbd by stdin's rule: interrupts on, only the foreground program
    (an SSH program its own terminal), a kill heard, a line at most. The
    shell's own reads of it (pour /dev/kbd) are unchanged.
  - scripts/devkbd-test.sh: a foreground spoon on /dev/kbd gets its
    typed line and Ctrl-C ends it; a background one writing /KEYS.TXT
    gets nothing typed at the shell after it; the shell answers after
    both. Before the fix the machine stopped at the first read.

v0.60.256 (2026-10-12)  — MINCECRAFT TESTS FAIL ON A MISSING STRETCH
  - mincecraft-play-test skipped its walk, jump and focus checks when no
    ticks were logged in that stretch, and passed (shown on a log with
    none); mincecraft-fly-test skipped its after-the-load check when the
    second boot had no mark (review R-T6). Each is a FAIL of its own now:
    at least two ticks in each stretch (46, 66 and 110 in a real run), a
    mark after the load.

v0.60.255 (2026-10-12)  — UNTIL AFTER KEYS WAITS FOR WHAT THE KEYS DO
  - qemu_keys' UNTIL searched the log from the last plain line typed, so
    after KEY: or TYPE: items a prompt printed before the keys satisfied
    it at once (bigdir-test's Tab, cont-test's up arrow; review R-T5).
    KEY: and TYPE: move its start now: what counts is what the log gets
    after them.
  - scripts/keys-until-test.sh, no QEMU: a fake monitor, found through its
    -serial file: argument as QEMU is, prints a prompt for a typed line at
    once and one for Tab (or a typed x) 2 s later; UNTIL after the keys
    must wait for the second. Before the fix it returned in 1.2-1.7 s.
    Every test in the full check still passes with the change.

v0.60.254 (2026-10-12)  — TWO MORE GATE CHECKS CAN FAIL
  - "ring3 sbrk syscall" took any TESTOUT heap= line, mise's own
    heap=FAIL included; it wants heap=AX, the pattern written and read
    back. "a wild fault still kills" took any drop.elf exit, a clean 0
    after "STILL ALIVE" included; it wants code -142, -(128 + 14), the
    page fault's kill (review R-T4).
  - gate-patterns-test has both failures and both real lines.

v0.60.253 (2026-10-12)  — DOOM-FB-TEST NEEDS DOOM
  - doom-fb-test compared the two builds' screens after a fixed wait with
    the serial thrown away, so two cleared consoles passed (99.81% with
    wash typed in place of doom; review R-T3). Each run's serial log is
    kept now: the screendump waits for "[doom] drawing at", which must say
    320x200 in mode 13h and 960x600 on the framebuffer, and the title must
    be at least half lit (it measures 93.2%). DOOM_FB_CMD types something
    else in place of doom; with wash the test fails.

v0.60.252 (2026-10-12)  — THE FULL CHECK REFUSES A STALE DISK
  - The tests boot copies of disk.img, and make never rebuilds it, so a
    change under user/ was tested only after rm disk.img && make disk by
    hand (review R-T2). check.sh now builds the programs after the default
    build and runs scripts/disk-fresh.sh: every program the disk recipe
    copies is read back out of the image and compared with the one just
    built; any that differ are named and the check stops there.
  - scripts/disk-fresh-test.sh: a fresh copy passes; one with SPOON.ELF
    replaced fails and names it. No QEMU.

v0.60.251 (2026-10-12)  — THE GATE'S PREEMPT CHECK CAN FAIL
  - "preempt_disable works" matched PASS anywhere in its log, so
    "[vmtest] PASS" satisfied it and "[pt] FAIL" did not stop it (review
    R-T1). It reads '^\[pt\] PASS' now, preempttest's own verdict.
  - scripts/gate-patterns-test.sh: a gate check, taken from its segment
    as written, runs against a log of the failure it is there to catch
    and must FAIL, and against the real success line and must pass. No
    QEMU. The next gate fixes add their cases to it.

v0.60.250 (2026-10-12)  — A TEXT FILE'S DOUBLE CLICK OPENS NEOSIM
  - In the files window a double click (or Enter) on a text file opens it
    in neosim, as the menu's "open in neosim" does: a terminal types
    cook neosim.elf PATH. jot stays a shell command; anything not text
    still goes to pour, a picture to a platter.
  - scripts/ctfiles-test.sh reads neosim's window now (its first rows,
    numbered) where it read jot's terminal, and quits each with :q.

v0.60.249 (2026-10-12)  — MINCECRAFT: FLYING
  - Space twice quickly (seven ticks, 350 ms) turns flying on or off, as
    Minecraft's creative mode: no fall, Space up and Shift down at a
    quarter of a block a tick, walking as before; coming down onto the
    ground ends it. A fall is counted afresh from where flying stops. One
    Space is still a jump. Markers: [mincecraft] fly on, fly off.
  - The world file is version 5: flying after the hearts; version 4 files
    load not flying.
  - scripts/mincecraft-fly-test.sh, the keys sent over QMP (downs and ups
    in real time): a jump, two quick taps (fly on), Space held (up 5
    blocks), let go (hanging still), Shift held (down 3), P; the next boot
    loads it still flying, hanging, not falling. The world-file tests
    (save, light, health, eat, fall, sun) read the 100-byte header now,
    and the save test loads a version 4 file too. SC_LSHIFT in ulib.h.

v0.60.248 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM: CTRL-A, CTRL-X
  - Ctrl-A adds the count (1 without) to the number under the cursor or
    the first after it on the line, Ctrl-X takes it away, as neovim's
    (nrformats bin,hex): a - just before the digits is its sign (-3 + 4
    is 1, 9 - 10 is -1); 0x numbers stay hex, as many digits as they had
    and their letters' case (0x0f + 1 is 0x10); the cursor goes to the
    number's last character; . repeats it, u takes it back. A line with no
    number at or after the cursor is left alone. Listed on :help.
  - scripts/neosim-incr-test.sh: 25 cases against neovim 0.12.5 (3 on the

v0.60.247 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM: RANGES, :g, :sort
  - Ranges before a command: % the whole text, N . $ a line, two of them
    with a comma (:2,4d :.,$s/a/b/ :3 to go there); :d deletes lines as dd
    does (the register too).
  - :g/pat/cmd, :g!/pat/cmd and :v/pat/cmd run cmd (d, s/../../, normal
    KEYS) on each line that matches (or not), the whole text unless a
    range says; the lines are marked first and the marks move as lines are
    put in or taken out, so a d does not skip the next one. :normal KEYS
    on its own runs the keys on each line of its range, an insert left
    open ended. Either is one undo step.
  - :sort, :sort! (reversed), n (by the first number on each line, lines
    without one first), u (one of each run alike); stable, so lines that
    sort alike keep their order, as neovim's. One undo step.
  - scripts/neosim-global-test.sh: 23 cases against neovim 0.12.5 (3 on
    the neosim before). Listed on :help.

v0.60.243 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM'S PATTERNS, AND RX.C SEARCHING ON
  - neosim's / ? n N * # and :s take vim's patterns ('magic'): . * ^ $
    [...] [^...] \< \> \w \s \d, \+ \? \= \| \( \) \{n,m\}; a bare + ? | ( ) {
    } is a character. They are turned into POSIX ERE and run by user/rx.c
    (its lists in an arena of neosim's, made again for each line). :s
    splits on an unescaped /, its replacement takes & and \1-\9, an empty
    match moves on a character. * and # search \<word\>, as neovim does.
  - Fixed in rx.c (sift -E, reckon, the shell's [[ =~ ]] too): a pattern
    that starts with an assertion (\< \> \b \B $) stopped looking at the
    first place the assertion was false with no other thread alive, so
    \<b in "foobaz barbaz" found nothing and $ from 0 found no end. It
    goes on to the next place now.
  - scripts/neosim-pattern-test.sh: 30 cases against neovim 0.12.5 (7 of
    30 on the neosim before). ere-test has four more patterns that start
    with an assertion (\<b z$ p\> \Ba): the old rx.c differs from GNU grep
    on them, this one does not.
  - The case generator doubles a key's backslash for vim's "..." string
    (\( had reached neovim as a plain (); no earlier case had one.

v0.60.241 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM: MACROS
  - q and a letter records every key typed until q in normal mode, into
    that register as text (so "ap shows it, Esc and all; qA adds to a);
    the command line says "recording @a" meanwhile, as neovim's. @ and the
    letter plays it, a count that many times, @@ the last one again.
  - A play is one undo step however many changes it made: while one plays,
    only its first change keeps a snapshot (an empty insert drops only a
    snapshot it made itself). After it, . is the macro's last change, not
    the @a.
  - scripts/neosim-macro-test.sh: 18 cases against neovim 0.12.5 (0 of 18
    on the neosim before). Listed on :help.

v0.60.235 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM: PARAGRAPHS
  - { and } go to the empty line before and after, as neovim's findpar
    (past the run of lines of the cursor's kind; at the text's end, its
    last character, taken). As motions they keep vim's exclusive-linewise
    rule: ending in column 0, whole lines when they start at or before the
    first non-blank (d} from a line's start), else up to the line before's
    end (d} from mid-line keeps the blank line).
  - ip ap: the paragraph's lines, or the run of blank lines the cursor is
    on; ap with the blank lines after (or before, when none are after),
    on blank lines them and the paragraph after; counts, visual vip vap.
    Listed on :help.

    62's note (no paragraphs) is done with it.

v0.60.218 (2026-10-12)  — NEOSIM: AUTO-INDENT AND %
  - Enter, o and O keep the line's indent (its leading spaces), as vim's
    autoindent; an indent nothing was typed after is taken back at Esc,
    and Enter on a line holding only its indent empties it and carries the
    indent on (the case neovim showed).
  - % goes to the matching ( [ { or ) ] }: the first bracket at or after
    the cursor on its line, then its match, nesting counted, across lines;
    a motion too (d%), inclusive.
  - scripts/neosim-indent-test.sh: 13 cases against neovim 0.12.5 (set
    autoindent expandtab: neovim's default would have made eight spaces a
    tab, neosim indents with spaces).

v0.60.206 (2026-10-11)  — NEOSIM: SEARCH, SUBSTITUTE, VISUAL, REPEAT
  - / and ? (plain text) from just past the cursor, round the end of the
    text once ("search hit BOTTOM, continuing at TOP"), E486 when nothing
    matches; n and N again, either way, with counts.
  - :s/A/B/ and :%s/A/B/g (plain text): the first match of each line or
    all of them, the whole job one undo step, the cursor to the last line
    changed, "N substitutions on M lines"; an empty A uses the last
    search. A line that would pass 255 is left as it was.
  - > and < (operators, and >> <<, counts too): four spaces in or out,
    empty lines left alone.
  - v and V: the motions move the cursor over a highlighted selection; d x
    y c > < take it (characters, across lines, or whole lines).
  - . does the last change again: the keys of each command that changed
    the text (an insert with what was typed, cw with its word) are kept;
    a visual one as V and j's (or v and l's), so as many lines from the
    cursor, as vim does.
  - scripts/neosim-find-test.sh: 30 cases against neovim 0.12.5 (sw=4
    et), as neosim-ops-test.

v0.60.205 (2026-10-11)  — NEOSIM: COUNTS, OPERATORS, UNDO
  - Counts before commands and motions (3j, 5x, 2dd, 2d3w); the motions
    w b e (vim's three classes: blanks, keyword characters, the rest; an
    empty line a word) 0 ^ $ gg G; the operators d c y with any of them,
    dd cc yy, D C, cw as ce on a word, an operator's w stopping at the end
    of the line. x X, p P (line-wise or character-wise, as the register
    was filled, counts too), J and 3J (one space, none before a ")", none
    onto an empty line).
  - u and Ctrl-R through whole-text snapshots taken before each change
    (an operator, x, p, J, a visit to insert mode; an empty visit leaves
    none) in a 1 MB arena for undo and 512 KB for redo, the oldest going
    when full. [+] goes when undo comes back to the text last written.
  - The line pool is 2048 lines now (4096 left no room under the 4 MB of
    pages a process may keep), the text, undo, register and window 3.3 MB.
  - scripts/neosim-ops-test.sh: 33 cases, each against neovim 0.12.5
    (nvim --clean --headless with the keys fed as typed, so undo steps are
    as typed; the expected files are kept in the test).

v0.60.204 (2026-10-11)  — NEOSIM: A MODAL EDITOR
  - neosim.elf (user/neosim/, its own Makefile rule): an editor after
    neovim in a window of its own, the user's name for it (it simmers).
    cook neosim.elf FILE. 80 x 28 cells of text in the kernel's 8x16 font
    with line numbers (the cursor's in amber) and ~ past the end, a status
    line (the mode, the file, [+] when changed, line:column) and a command
    line. Normal mode: h j k l and the arrows, i a A I o O, x, :. Insert
    mode: typing, Enter, Backspace (joining lines), Tab as four spaces,
    the arrows, Esc (the cursor back one, as vim's). Commands: :w [FILE],
    :q (refused with changes, vim's E37 on the command line), :q!, :wq,
    :x, :e[!] FILE. A missing file opens empty and is made at :w; lines
    over 255 are cut at load, said so. The cursor is an underline in the
    font's two empty rows, so it never hides a character.
  - ulib has KEY_UP and the other codes past 255 that WEV_KEY carries.
  - scripts/neosim-test.sh: a scripted edit (A O o x I, :wq) of a two-line
    file against the expected bytes; the status line and the numbered
    lines read back from screendumps against the font; :q refused and
    said, :q! leaving the file as it was.

v0.60.191 (2026-10-11)  — HOME FOR EVERY PROGRAM
  - build_env hands HOME, the cook's home bowl (/ for the headchef,
    /home/<cook> for the rest), ahead of what the cook handed, unless the
    cook handed a HOME of their own. Programs had no way to find it (no
    who-am-I call, and a relative open is taken from /); Mincecraft saves
    its world there. home_of() is shared with ensure_home.
  - scripts/env-test.sh: the whole-environment listings keep to the S
    variables (sift.elf S), as bash's side always did; HOME checked as
    the headchef, a handed HOME=/elsewhere, after discarding it, and as
    an ordinary cook (saucier: /home/saucier).

v0.60.190 (2026-10-11)  — HELD KEYS FOR A PROGRAM'S WINDOW
  - SYS_WIN_KEYS (24): the keys held now, as a 32-byte bitmap (scancode
    n at bit n, extended keys at 0x80|code), for the caller's own window
    only while it is placed, the focus and the cloche is up; otherwise
    all zeros. The lock check matters: key_state sees what is typed at
    the cloche too, so without it a program could read the secret. For
    Mincecraft, which walks while W is held; windows only get presses.
    ulib: sys_win_keys, key_held, SC_* scancodes.
  - Found by its test: key_state held F7 (0x41) from boot on, forever.
    mouse_init read the controller's config byte (0x41) with IRQ 1 live,
    and the latched IRQ 1 then read port 0x60, empty but still giving
    its last byte. mouse_init now talks to the controller with
    interrupts off, and the keyboard's handler reads only a byte the
    status says is there and the keyboard's (not the mouse's).
  - prod.elf keys: the limits (a bad id, another program's, a pointer it
    never had), then the held keys whenever they change.
  - scripts/winkeys-test.sh: W held a second with prod keys on top (seen),
    after Alt+Tab elsewhere (not), at the cloche with the secret typed
    (neither), and on top again (seen); no key nobody pressed.

v0.60.183 (2026-10-09)  — CLOSING THE KITCHEN FROM THE DESKTOP
  - The Kitchen menu gains "close up" (power off) and "reheat" (restart),
    the headchef's only, as freeze and reheat are at the prompt; anyone
    else is told so on the bar, and it is logged. Every terminal is hung
    up first and its session given up to 2 s to end, which saves its
    leftovers; then the power goes.
  - src/power.c: power_off() writes SLP_EN to the PIIX4's PM1a control
    port (0x604, where SeaBIOS puts it; QEMU's DSDT gives S5 sleep type
    0), then Bochs's 0xB004, then halts as freeze does; measured on
    QEMU's i440fx machine, the first write is enough and QEMU exits.
    power_reboot() is reheat's reset, moved there.
  - Not done: the clock-in screen offering it. Nobody is clocked in
    there, and it is the headchef's only; clock in and use the menu.
  - scripts/ctcloseup-test.sh: as the headchef, `whoami` in a terminal,
    then close up: QEMU (without -no-shutdown) exits by itself and
    /etc/LEFTOVER holds whoami; as saucier refused, said on the bar,
    QEMU running; reheat (without -no-reboot) boots to the login again.
    Six-wide soak 6 of 6.

  - A "picture <path>" line in the cook's `countertop` settings names a

v0.60.180 (2026-10-09)  — CPU USE IN EXPO
  - SYS_PROCS gives each process's CPU (uproc_t.cpu, appended): its

    percent, capped at 100. expo shows it right-aligned in a CPU column;

v0.60.179 (2026-10-09)  — STRAIGHT TO THE DESKTOP
  - Asked for by the user: booted with QEMU's

    prompt. The shell's callback checks the secret and does what a

  - clockout's wipe of the console's shell is clockout_wipe(), shared.

v0.60.173 (2026-10-09)  — scale.elf: A CALCULATOR
  - Buttons clicked or keys typed (digits . + - x or * / = or Enter, C),
    left to right as a pocket calculator. No FPU in ring 3: 64-bit
    millionths, six decimals with trailing zeros dropped; past 10^12 or a
    division by 0 the display says error until C. Multiplication splits
    both sides into whole and fraction and checks each product against
    the limit; division is long division a decimal at a time, so neither
    passes 64 bits (fuzzed on the host against exact integers: 20000
    cases, 0 different). The display is the kernel's font, doubled. In
    the Kitchen menu.
  - scripts/ctscale-test.sh: 12.5x4=50, 7/2=3.5, 1/0 an error, 2+3x4=20
    typed; C 9 x 9 = 81 clicked; the display read back. ctsettings-test
    finds backdrop a row lower. Six-wide soak 6 of 6.

v0.60.171 (2026-10-09)  — KEYBOARD SHORTCUTS
  - F1-F12 are key codes now (KEY_F1.. KEY_F12, 265-276): the scancode
    table has 0 for them, so they reached no one. Consumers that take
    only printable characters ignore them, as they do the arrows.
  - On the desktop: Ctrl+Alt+T opens a terminal; Alt+F4 closes the
    focused window as its close box would (a program's gets WEV_CLOSE
    and decides).
  - The Alt combinations (and Alt+Tab, v0.60.155) are decided in the
    keyboard IRQ when the key goes down: KEY_ALT_TAB, KEY_ALT_F4,
    KEY_CTRL_ALT_T. First built reading Alt's state when the desktop
    took the key, as Alt+Tab did: a six-wide soak lost one Alt+F4 in six
    (Alt already up by then). Twelve at once after: 12 of 12. Alt+Tab
    at the console no longer types a tab.
  - scripts/ctkeys-test.sh: Ctrl+Alt+T (a command typed there), Alt+F4
    on the terminal and then Pantry, Ctrl+Alt+T again, frost asked to
    close by Alt+F4 and closing; the log and a screendump.

  - SYS_PROCS (21): the live processes, by pid: pid, program, cook (uid

v0.60.166 (2026-10-09)  — SCROLLBACK IN TERMINAL WINDOWS
  - A terminal window keeps the last 300 lines that scroll off its top

    back through them three lines a notch; typing, or new output, puts

v0.60.152 (2026-10-09)  — HOW FAST A PROGRAM CAN DRAW (MEASURED)
  - sear.elf W H N puts N frames and times its fill and its puts with
    rdtsc against the ticks; the desktop now logs, when a program's

      640x480: put 1748 / 3149 us (555 / 312 puts a second), a draw
               (repaint and copy out) 814 / 825 us
      320x240: put 419 / 855 us, a draw 478 / 405 us
    The desktop shows 45-50 frames a second either way, held there by
    its 15 ms nap, keeping the newest put and dropping the rest. The
    queue item's premise (a whole-body put too slow for 30 a second)
    measures false, so no put of a rectangle was built. The put is a
    plain memcpy of up to 1.2 MB at ~0.7 GB/s; there is room there if
    it is ever wanted.

v0.60.151 (2026-10-09)  — THE WHEEL
  - mouse_init knocks as an IntelliMouse does (sample rates 200, 100, 80,

    the wheel. A mouse that stays 0 keeps three. mouse_wheel() is the
    notches since boot, down positive, for the desktop to difference.

    stopping at the top and with the last entry on the last row.

v0.60.150 (2026-10-09)  — THE WINDOW CALLS, ATTACKED
  - prod.elf victim, started at the console in the background, waits
    for the desktop and opens a green window; limits then runs in a
    terminal on the desktop, its answers to a file (run first from the
    console too, it raced the victim to the desktop under load and won,
    attacking ids before the victim had one). limits asks for windows of 0 and
    641 wide, 481 high, -1; with a title in the kernel and one running
    off the end of its memory; puts pixels from above its break, the
    kernel, half past its break; asks for events into the kernel and
    above its break; uses ids 8, -1 and all seven that are not its own
    (the victim's among them). All give -1, and the victim's window
    stays green.
  - Fixed: a second SYS_WIN_CLOSE of the same window gave 0 (the slot
    waits for the desktop's loop to free it). It gives -1 now.
  - Once the desktop has left, the victim's put, event and open give -1,
    and its close gives 0 and frees the slot.
  - scripts/prodwin-test.sh (it checks the victim's window was up before
    limits asked for one); six-wide soak 6 of 6.

v0.60.149 (2026-10-09)  — A POINTER A PROGRAM NEVER HAD (FIX)
  - Any program could panic the machine: user_ok checked only that a
    pointer was inside the user range, so SYS_WRITE from 0xC6000000
    (above the break) or 0xC2000000 (between the image and the heap),
    and SYS_READ or SYS_STAT into 0xC6000000, faulted in the kernel's
    own copy; the demand pager declined and isr.c panicked. All four
    did, proven with prod.elf before the fix.
  - user_ok now also wants every page: mapped, or one the pager would
    give on first touch (the heap below the break, the stack above its
    guard). Such a call gives -1. Checked before the copy, not caught in
    the fault: by then the call may hold FAT's or the desktop's lock.
  - prod.elf (a poke with a fork) and scripts/prod-test.sh: all four
    give -1, the program ends normally, no panic, the shell goes on.

v0.60.148 (2026-10-09)  — TALLY WITH DECIMALS (seq)
  - FIRST, STEP and LAST may have fractions (0.5, .5, 1., -.25). No
    floating point in ring 3, so each is a 64-bit integer in units of the
    finest fraction typed; every value is FIRST plus whole STEPs, exact,
    and stops past LAST. Printed with as many decimals as FIRST or STEP
    has, LAST's not counted (seq 1 1.50 prints 1), as GNU seq does. -w
    is seq's arithmetic on the typed strings ("1." one narrower, ".5" one
    wider, each widened or narrowed to the printed decimals). 17 digits
    at most once scaled; -.5 is a number, not an option.
  - seq-test.sh: 36 more forms against GNU seq, 57 in all (negatives,
    steps that miss LAST, counting down, -w and -s with decimals, 3001
    lines of 0.001). They now run from one script on the disk (. /SEQ.SH):
    typing 57 lines and waiting for each prompt took 188 s, the script
    takes 7.

v0.60.147 (2026-10-09)  — LAYER -s, AND - MORE THAN ONCE (paste)
  - Every - is now the same stdin, so `layer.elf - -` takes its lines in
    turn as paste does. Each - had its own 2 KB buffer, so the first read
    the lot: `spoon.elf N.TXT | layer.elf - -` gave "1<TAB>" on each of
    five lines, not "1<TAB>2", "3<TAB>4", "5<TAB>".
  - -s (serial): each file's lines on one line, the -d list starting over
    for each file, an empty file giving an empty line. Options cluster
    (-sd:) and -- ends them.
  - layer-test.sh: nine more forms against GNU paste byte for byte (- -,
    - FILE -, - - - past a read, -s over uneven and empty files, -s -d,
    -sd:, -s with - twice, -s past a read, -d '\0' -s); 19 in all.

v0.60.146 (2026-10-09)  — COUNTERTOP: WINDOWS FOR PROGRAMS (STEP 7)
  - SYS_WIN_OPEN (a width, height and title: an id), SYS_WIN_PUT (the

v0.60.143 (2026-10-09)  — COUNTERTOP: TERMINAL WINDOWS (STEP 4)
  - The bar has Terminal and Leave buttons. Terminal opens a window with a
    shell of its own: a wterm_t (a term_t of 80x25 cells with a key ring)
    and shell_session_start on it, as the SSH server gives each channel,
    so each window has its own task, cook, cwd, history and jobs. Its
    output lands in its cells and the window is drawn again; the cursor
    shows in the focused one.

    program); Esc leaves only when no terminal has the focus. clockout or
    exit in a terminal closes its window; its close box hangs the session
    up (programs killed, getc -1); its memory goes once its task is done.
    Leave hangs every session up and waits for them.

    serial markers included).

v0.60.141 (2026-10-09)  — COUNTERTOP: THE DESKTOP'S SURFACE (STEP 2)
  - `countertop` (src/countertop.c) takes the framebuffer as mode 13h
    programs do and gives it back on Esc: a blue-to-orange gradient, a bar
    along the top with "soupOS countertop" and the RTC's time ticking, and
    an arrow pointer that follows the mouse in pixels.
  - Measured first: the back buffer is 3 MB, most of the 8 MB heap. A
    1024-pixel row is exactly a 4 KB page and RAM is identity-mapped, so it
    is 768 pages from the physical allocator behind an array of row
    pointers (of ~29 850 free), the heap untouched. Drawing goes to it; one
    dirty rectangle goes out to the framebuffer; the pointer is laid over
    the rows on their way out, never drawn into the buffer, so nothing
    under it is saved or restored.
  - scripts/countertop-test.sh reads screendumps: the bar, "soupOS" glyph
    for glyph against font8x16, the gradient's exact colours, the pointer's
    outline and fill before and after a move with nothing left behind, the
    console back after Esc.

v0.60.139 (2026-10-09)  — PAIR -L AND -S
  - pair.elf -l every differing byte (its number right-aligned to the
    digits of the smaller file's size, both values " %3o"), an EOF after
    them said without its line; -s nothing said, only the status; -ls a
    usage error, 2. (The EOF message itself was GNU's since v0.59.4.)
  - Found on the way: GNU cmp 3.12 -l exits 0 when the last buffer it
    compares of a file over ~16 KB matches, though bytes before differed.
    pair says 1 whenever one differed; the test puts a difference near the
    end so the two agree.
  - scripts/cmpls-test.sh compares ten cases with GNU cmp, statuses
    included.

v0.60.138 (2026-10-09)  — LABEL: NL'S OPTIONS
  - label.elf -b a|t|n, -w WIDTH, -s SEP, -v START, -i STEP, -n ln|rn|rz,
    as GNU nl's, a value with its option or after it; a line not
    numbered gets WIDTH and SEP's worth of spaces. label still numbers

v0.60.137 (2026-10-09)  — TAKE -D DELIM (READ -D)
  - take -d DELIM reads up to DELIM's first character instead of a
    newline (-d '' to the end of the input); a newline then is data, and
    parts words as IFS's does; the end of input before DELIM sets the
    names and gives status 1, as bash's. With -r -a -n -p, from a pipe,
    <<< and < FILE. take_line's delimiter is set for that one read, so
    stock, runner and select still read lines.
  - scripts/taked-test.sh compares nine lines with bash's read -d, and a
    pipe.

v0.60.136 (2026-10-09)  — FOR (( INIT ; COND ; STEP ))
  - bash's arithmetic for loop: INIT once, COND before each turn (empty is
    true), STEP after each (a continue's too), each part expanded when it
    is used; break, continue and their N; the status the body's last, 0
    if it never ran. for(( with no space is made for (( as a line comes
    in, so every reader after sees one form.
  - scripts/cfor-test.sh compares 36 lines with bash, a recipe over
    several lines included.

v0.60.135 (2026-10-09)  — WEIGH OVER SEVERAL FILES, AND -L
  - weigh.elf FILE...: a line each and a `total` line, every count padded
    to the digits of the total bytes (with one count or several), as GNU
    wc; -L the longest line (tabs to the next multiple of 8), its total
    the longest of all. A file that will not open is said, the rest
    counted, status 1. One file prints as before; the WCOUT marker the
    gate reads is the total's.
  - scripts/weighfiles-test.sh compares 12 cases and stdin, statuses

v0.60.134 (2026-10-09)  — SIFT OVER SEVERAL FILES, -L -H -H -W -X
  - sift.elf PATTERN FILE...: each line, -o match and -c count after
    NAME: when there is more than one file (-H always, -h never); -l the
    names of the files with a selected line, each once (reading stops at
    the first); -w a whole-word match (a later one tried when the first is
    not), -x the whole line (^( )$ round an -E pattern). A file that will
    not open is said and the rest read; status 2. Stdin is
    "(standard input)" to -l.
  - scripts/siftfiles-test.sh compares 17 cases and stdin, statuses
    included, with GNU grep; every earlier sift test still passes.

v0.60.133 (2026-10-09)  — RACK -F AND -B
  - rack.elf -f folds lower case to upper when comparing keys (so _ and [
    sort among the letters as GNU's do), -b skips blanks at the start of
    each key (the whole line's without -k); with -r -n -u -k -t as they
    were. The last-resort whole-line comparison stays plain bytes.
  - scripts/rackfb-test.sh compares 12 cases with GNU sort, LC_ALL=C.

v0.60.132 (2026-10-09)  — SWAP [:CLASS:], OCTAL AND -C
  - swap.elf sets take [:alpha:] [:digit:] [:alnum:] [:upper:] [:lower:]
    [:space:] [:punct:] [:blank:] [:xdigit:] [:cntrl:] [:print:]
    [:graph:], each in byte order, so [:lower:] [:upper:] translates
    letter for letter; \NNN octal and \\ \a \b \f \r \v beside \n \t;
    -c (or -C) takes every byte not in the first set, for -d, -s and
    translating, as GNU tr's.
  - scripts/swapclass-test.sh compares 16 cases with GNU tr, byte for byte,
    high and control bytes in the input.

v0.60.131 (2026-10-09)  — EXPIRY +FORMAT AND -d @SECONDS
  - expiry +FORMAT is date's format: %Y %m %d %H %M %S %y %C %e %k %l %j
    %I %p %a %A %b %h %B %u %w %s %Z %F %T %D %R %n %t %%, anything else
    after a % as written, in the C locale, for the RTC's time (UTC).
    -d @SECONDS formats that moment instead, as GNU date's; with no
    format, date's own. Bare expiry prints what it always did.
  - Days to and from a calendar date by the civil-from-days algorithm, so
    1969, leap days, 2100 and past 2^31 all come out right.
  - scripts/expiry-test.sh compares 40 lines at 10 moments with GNU date
    -u, and the RTC's %s with the host's clock.

v0.60.130 (2026-10-09)  — GLOBS IN BUILTINS' WORDS
  - Measured: only a program's words (cook) and a for loop's were ever
    globbed; `slurp /SP*.ELF`, `pour /SPOT*` and a bare $x holding a
    pattern all got the pattern itself. (Queue 46 item 1 took this for a
    big glob that did not fit the line; it was every glob in a builtin.)
  - Now a builtin's or a function's unquoted words with * ? or [...] are
    their matches, sorted, a name with a blank kept one word; no match
    leaves the word; NAME=value words and cook lines are left to what
    they were; [ and ] alone are not globs. A result too long for the
    line is said and not run (status 1), never the pattern instead.
  - scripts/bglob-test.sh compares 16 lines with bash, and the 302-name
    glob's refusal.

v0.60.129 (2026-10-09)  — CULL -U AND -I
  - cull.elf -u prints only the lines that are not repeated, -i compares
    without case (the first of each run printed), with -c and -d as
    before; -d with -u prints nothing, as GNU uniq's.
  - scripts/cullui-test.sh compares eleven cases with GNU uniq.

v0.60.128 (2026-10-09)  — SKIM AND DREGS: BYTES, -N AND +N
  - skim.elf -c N (the first N bytes), -n -N and -c -N (all but the last
    N lines or bytes, read whole first); dregs.elf -c N (the last N bytes,
    keeping no more than that), -n +N and -c +N (from line or byte N on,

    headtail-test.sh by mistake; restored from git before the commit.)

v0.60.127 (2026-10-09)  — SLICE -C, -B AND -S
  - slice.elf -c LIST (and -b, the same where a character is a byte)
    picks characters by position with -f's lists (N, N-M, N-, -M, in the
    line's order, each once); -s with -f leaves out lines with no
    delimiter, as GNU cut's. Positions and fields up to 4096.
  - scripts/slicec-test.sh compares 13 cases with GNU cut.

v0.60.126 (2026-10-09)  — $'...' (ANSI-C QUOTING)
  - bash's $'...': \n \t \r \\ \' \" \? \a \b \e \f \v, \NNN octal,
    \xHH hex, \cX a control, an unknown escape kept as typed, a NUL ending
    it, nothing else expanded inside; literal inside "...".
  - Each $'...' becomes '...' as a line comes into run_line, so every
    reader after (splitter, redirections, pipes, [[ ]], case) sees plain
    single quotes and none has to know \' does not close it. (A first try
    decoded it only in expansion: a > after $'it\'s' was taken as text.)
  - Bytes 1, 2 and 31 cannot be made this way: the shell uses them as its
    own markers (here-documents, array elements).
  - scripts/ansic-test.sh compares 18 lines with bash byte for byte, and
    one typed at the prompt with a redirection.

v0.60.124 (2026-10-09)  — RECKON.ELF (EXPR)
  - reckon.elf is expr under a kitchen name, with GNU's precedence and
    rules: | and & (and their null-or-0 tests, both null giving 0),
    comparisons as numbers when both are and as text otherwise, + - * / %
    on 64-bit integers, STRING : REGEX, length, substr, index, match,
    + TOKEN, ( ). Status 0, 1 for a null or 0 result, 2 for errors.
  - : takes a basic regex, as expr's, turned into rx.c's extended one
    (\( \| \+ are operators, ( | + plain, * first plain, ^ only first and
    $ only last), anchored at the start; with a group the result is what
    it matched, without, how much matched.
  - scripts/reckon-test.sh compares 52 expressions' output and status
    with GNU expr.
  - ls-test.sh waits for the prompt after each listing, not two seconds:
    under a loaded full check the listing ran past the wait. (Blamed here
    at first on SYS_READDIR reading the directory once per entry; measured
    in queue 45, the whole listing takes 40 to 90 ms, so it was the load.)
    The flake is logged.

v0.60.122 (2026-10-09)  — SPOT.ELF (DIFF)
  - spot.elf OLD NEW is diff under a kitchen name, in GNU's normal

    status 0/1/2.
  - Which lines it calls changed is GNU's choice, so its way of choosing
    is GNU's, ported step for step: the shared start and end set aside,
    discard_confusing_lines, Myers' middle snake (compareseq, diag), and

v0.60.121 (2026-10-09)  — TAKE -A AND STOCK (READ -A, MAPFILE)
  - take -a NAME puts a line's words in the array NAME (with -r and the
    rest of take's options).
  - stock [-t] [NAME] is bash's mapfile under a kitchen name: every line
    of its input an element of NAME (MAPFILE when not given), newlines
    kept unless -t. An array is still one value of 256 bytes: what does
    not fit is left off, status 1.
  - Both read a pipe, as runner does (X | stock NAME, X | take A B):
    unlike bash, where a pipe's last command is a subshell and loses
    them, the values stay.
  - set_array() writes an array whole, for these and BASH_REMATCH.
  - scripts/stock-test.sh compares nine lines with bash's read -a and

v0.60.120 (2026-10-09)  — [[ STRING =~ REGEX ]] AND BASH_REMATCH
  - =~ in [[ ]] (left out in v0.60.113) through rx.c, built into the
    kernel's shell: status 0 a match, 1 none, 2 a bad regex (said
    nothing, as bash). BASH_REMATCH is an array: the match, then each
    group (empty for one that took no part); emptied when nothing matches.
    Quoted pieces of the regex are text, unquoted ones regex, a variable's
    value included; the word after =~ keeps its ( ) and the blanks inside
    them, as bash reads it.
  - scripts/rematch-test.sh compares 13 lines with bash.

v0.60.119 (2026-10-09)  — REGULAR EXPRESSIONS: RX.C AND SIFT -E
  - user/rx.c: POSIX extended regular expressions in one file the
    programs and the kernel can both include (each says how to allocate).
    Literals, ., [...] with ranges, [^...] and [:class:], * + ? {m,n}, ^ $,
    |, ( ) with nine groups saved, \w \W \s \S \b \B \< \>, and GNU's
    quirks (a leading * repeats nothing; a ) with no ( and a { that is not
    a count are literal). Compiled to a small program and run as a Pike
    VM, so time is text times pattern and nothing can make it hang;
    leftmost-longest, as grep -o reports; no recursion in the matcher.
  - sift.elf -E PATTERN uses it, with -v -c -i -n -o as before; a bad
    pattern is status 2.
  - scripts/ere-test.sh compares 119 runs (29 patterns, four modes each,
    three exponential for backtracking, three bad) with GNU grep -E.

v0.60.118 (2026-10-09)  — TUMBLE.ELF (SHUF) AND SYS_RANDOM
  - SYS_RANDOM (15): up to 256 bytes a call from the kernel's entropy pool
    (what $RANDOM draws from) into a buffer the program owns; ulib's
    sys_random. Ring 3 had no randomness before.
  - tumble.elf [-n N] [-i LO-HI | FILE] is shuf under a kitchen name:
    lines of a file or stdin in a random order, every order as likely
    (Fisher-Yates, the uneven top of each draw thrown back), N of them with
    -n, the numbers LO..HI with -i; a bad range or count is status 1.
  - scripts/tumble-test.sh checks properties, not bytes: permutations,
    two runs differ, -n, -i, empty, and over 200 draws each of four lines
    comes first a fair share (46/44/56/54 here).

v0.60.117 (2026-10-09)  — RUNNER (XARGS)
  - X | runner [-n N] [-I R] [-r] CMD is xargs under a kitchen name (the
    one who carries the plates): X's words (quotes and \ as xargs reads
    them) added to CMD, as many as fit a line (and a program's 128 bytes
    of arguments), N with -n; -I R once a line with R replaced. Each word
    goes in quoted. No input still runs CMD once unless -r; status 123 if
    a run failed, 127 if CMD is not there, 1 at an unmatched quote (what
    came before still runs), as GNU's.
  - A pipe into runner feeds it as a pipe feeds a while loop (compound_bar
    and compound_end know it), so it reads X with take's reader.
  - scripts/runner-test.sh compares 18 lines with GNU xargs, and 100 words
    through its batches.

v0.60.116 (2026-10-09)  — RAIL (JOBS)
  - rail [-p] [%N] is bash's jobs under a kitchen name (the rail the order
    tickets hang on): [N], + the newest and - the one before, Running,
    Stopped, Done, Exit N, Killed, and the command, & while it runs. A
    finished one is shown once and then collected, its status kept for
    rest as the prompt keeps it. -p the PIDs; %9 no such job, status 1.
  - Each job now keeps what it was ("cook ..." as given) beside its PID
    and number.
  - scripts/rail-test.sh compares eleven lines with bash's jobs under
    set -m, a Ctrl-Z'd job included.

v0.60.115 (2026-10-09)  — TEMPER (SET)
  - temper is sh's set under a kitchen name. -e stops a recipe at a
    failure (what follow -e did, now from inside), -u makes an unset $NAME
    or $N an error ("NAME: unbound variable", not run, status 1, a recipe
    stops; ${x:-y} is fine), -x writes + and each command as it will run,
    words quoted as bash quotes them, past any $( ) capture to the
    terminal; + turns each off. temper -- W.. and temper W.. set $1.. and
    $#; bare temper lists the variables, as set does.
  - A recipe starts with -u and -x off and leaves them as they were, as a
    new sh does; a . file shares them.
  - scripts/temper-test.sh compares 13 lines and the -x trace with bash.

v0.60.114 (2026-10-09)  — function NAME { ... }
  - bash's other way to write a function (a keyword, its own name):
    function NAME { ... } and function NAME() { ... }, made NAME() { ... }
    wherever a command starts, so one line, several typed lines (the >
    prompt now counts the { after function NAME) and recipes all work.
  - scripts/fnkw-test.sh compares nine lines with bash.

v0.60.113 (2026-10-09)  — [[ EXPR ]]
  - bash's conditional command (a keyword, so its own name). Its words are
    taken before any expansion, so && || < > ( ) inside are its own; each
    operand is expanded alone, only when reached, and never split. == and
    != match a pattern, quoted pieces as text ("a"* is a then anything);
    < > compare text; ! ( ) && || group; -f -z -eq and the rest are
    taste's. Status 2 for a malformed one or no ]].
  - =~ is left out (sift has no regular expressions): it says so, 2.
  - scripts/dbracket-test.sh compares 25 lines with bash.

v0.60.112 (2026-10-09)  — PORTION.ELF (FACTOR)
  - portion.elf N... is factor under a kitchen name: N, a colon, its
    primes in order, from the words or stdin, up to 2^64-1 (GNU's goes
    past with big numbers; portion says 2^64 is not one, status 1). Trial
    division, Miller-Rabin with the twelve bases exact below 2^64, and
    Pollard-Brent rho: two 32-bit primes multiplied take 0.15 s.
  - User programs link libgcc for 64-bit division, as the kernel does;
    only what a program calls is pulled in, and the other 46 programs
    built byte for byte the same.
  - scripts/portion-test.sh compares 16 numbers, the hard product, stdin
    and bad input with the host's factor.

v0.60.104 (2026-10-09)  — :, FRESH AND SPOILED
  - `:` (POSIX's special builtin, so it keeps its name) does nothing,

    empties F, `while :` loops. fresh and spoiled are sh's true and false

v0.60.103 (2026-10-09)  — SELECT
  - `select NAME in WORDS ; do ... ; done` is sh's: the words numbered,
    the $PS3 prompt (or "#? "), a line read from the terminal or what the
    loop is fed (done < F, X | select); NAME the word with that number,
    empty if none, REPLY the line; a blank line shows the menu again; round
    until break, or the end of input (status 1, as bash). A loop in every
    way the others are: match_done, compound_end, compound_bar, follow's
    joining, and inspect's and plain's keywords.
  - scripts/select-test.sh compares a file-fed select with bash and types
    one with $PS3 and break.

v0.60.102 (2026-10-09)  — TAB FINISHES $NAME
  - A word whose last $ (or ${) is followed by name characters finishes
    from the shell's variable names: one fit is finished (and closed with
    } after ${), several as far as they agree and listed on the second
    Tab, as bash's completion does. A name's case is its own: $mi does

v0.60.101 (2026-10-09)  — REST -N, MARINATE.ELF AND SYS_SLEEP
  - `rest -n` is wait -n: the next of this shell's background jobs to end,
    whichever it is, and its status; none, 127; Ctrl-C, 130.
  - SYS_SLEEP (14): a program's task sleeps, ten seconds at most a call;
    ulib's sys_sleep. It turns interrupts back on first, as SYS_YIELD does
    (the int 0x80 gate clears IF, and only the timer wakes a sleeper: the
    first try hung for that).
  - marinate.elf SECONDS (tenths too) is sleep under a kitchen name, a
    tenth of a second a step so a kill lands within one; a word is
    status 2.
  - scripts/restn-test.sh checks rest -n's order and statuses and that it
    waits for the quicker of two jobs, and times marinate.
  - qemu_keys.py: UNTIL now counts only what comes after the typed line's
    own echo. Under a loaded full check heredoc-test typed its line while
    the login was still being checked, and the prompt printed after the
    login (before the line ran) satisfied UNTIL; QEMU was killed with the
    script half run. A line that never echoes (a secret) falls back to
    the old rule after 10 s. Every typed test gets it.

v0.60.100 (2026-10-09)  — PLAIN (COMMAND)
  - `plain CMD [ARGS]` is sh's command under a kitchen name: CMD runs as
    the builtin or the program it names, never a function of that name
    (a nickname is only ever the typed first word's), so
    `slurp() { plain slurp "[$@]" ; }` wraps the builtin and still calls
    it. A program is found as cook finds it; none is 127. `plain -v NAME`
    says what NAME would run, as command -v: a keyword's, builtin's or
    function's name, a nickname's definition, a program's path, nothing
    and status 1 for none.
  - scripts/plain-test.sh compares four files and five lines with bash.

v0.60.99 (2026-10-09)  — SHELVE, UNSHELVE, SHELF (PUSHD, POPD, DIRS)
  - `shelve BOWL` goes there and keeps where it was on a stack (alone it
    swaps with the top), `unshelve` goes back to the top and takes it off
    (status 1 on an empty shelf), `shelf` lists the bowl it is in and then
    the stack, `shelf -c` clears it; each says the stack after, as bash's
    pushd, popd and dirs. Eight deep; no ~ for home (the headchef's is /).
    A clockout clears it.
  - scripts/shelf-test.sh compares two files and two statuses with bash.

v0.60.98 (2026-10-09)  — ${!A[@]} AND ${!PREFIX*}
  - ${!a[@]} and ${!a[*]} are an array's indexes (0 up, the arrays being
    dense; a plain variable's is 0), ${!p*} and ${!p@} the names of the
    variables starting with p, by name; "${!..@}" a word each, "${!..*}"
    one, as bash.
  - scripts/keys-test.sh compares eight lines with bash.

v0.60.97 (2026-10-09)  — CASE ;& AND ;;&
  - An arm ended by ;& runs the next arm's commands too, untested, and on
    down while they end so; one ended by ;;& goes on testing the arms
    after it. ;; stops, as before. On a line, in a loop and in a script's
    case over lines.
  - scripts/casefall-test.sh compares six lines and a script with bash.
  - x25519-test.sh measures again, once, when its thousand rounds go over

v0.60.96 (2026-10-09)  — TAKE -P, -N, -T
  - take learns read's options: -p PROMPT shown before it reads (not when
    the input is a pipe), -n N for N characters and no more (no Enter
    needed, no \ joining), -t SECS (to the tenth) to give up at, with
